ANNOTATIONS. 179

For [the matter] cannot dye the body by itself, nor separate itself, unless the spirit, which lies hidden within, emerges. For how can a body exist without a spirit, except through that beautiful nature which is in it—namely, the **animal spirit** <note>In alchemy, the "animal spirit" refers to the vital, volatile principle that gives life and movement to matter.</note>—which draws out and carries in the color of the **tincture** <note>The coloring agent that transforms the metal.</note>.

Therefore, **Venus** <note>The alchemical name for copper.</note> does not dye, but is dyed. For the earthy part of the matter, being coarse, does not dye; rather, that quality of thin substance produces the tincture in the body. When **Venus**, that is, the body, is rightly governed by **magnesia** <term>Magnesia: Not the modern chemical, but a generic alchemical term for a "primitive matter" or a specific mineral blend used to draw out the spirits of metals.</term>, and brings its thin substance into the light, then at last it assumes the power of dyeing, and it dyes.

Moreover, **Venus** consists of four bodies. Indeed, the species of tincture are called "solids," "liquids," and "herbs" by way of likeness: the solids range from the **cloud** <note>Likely referring to a vaporous or powdered state.</note> to **chrysocolla** <term>Chrysocolla: From the Greek for "gold-glue," it refers here to a mineral used as a flux or a coloring agent in the transformation of base metals.</term>, while the liquids are the **divine water**. For divine things are retained by the yellow ones, etc.

This, however, is the truth: *Nature rejoices in nature, and nature embraces nature.* <note>original: "Natura naturam oblectat, & natura naturam complectitur." This is one of the most famous axioms in alchemy, attributed to the ancient sage Democritus.</note>

Again, the four-bodied nature of **Venus** is shown through magnesia, and the "herbs" of many-colored species come into the light through the grinding <note>original: "attritione"</note> in the composition of the elements. When the grindings of the bodies are done reasonably, they then flourish with a blooming green—though they are not truly herbs except in color alone—nor can they remain in the fire.

For he speaks of "solids," "liquids," and "herbs." He calls "solids" those bodies that penetrate **whiteness** <term>Albedo: The second major stage of the alchemical "Great Work," signifying purification and the preparation for the gold-making stage.</term> and **yellowing** <term>Citrinitas: The stage of "yellowing" that follows the white stage, indicating the approach of the final "Red" goal.</term>, remaining in the bodies, not yielding at all, but firmly retaining and overcoming them. He spoke of "liquids" so that he might restore them through moist things, that is, through the **divine water** <term>Divine Water: Also called "sulfur water" (theion hydor), it was a sharp, transformative liquid—often containing mercury or sulfur—used to dissolve and "spiritualize" metals.</term>. What, then, is this divine water, and why do they call it "divine"? Certainly a great...

->### OF NICHOLAS FLAMEL<-

This name [is given] above all others, and more excellently, because without this [water] nothing is produced or perfected in the work. Divine water is that which makes the nature simple, most bright, most pure, and without the shadow of bodies, as if changing the bodies and wiping them clean; it is that which extracts the hidden nature from the bodies. For once joined to the form of nature, it effects and works a most beautiful task through nature. It is called sharp water, elevated and summoned forth. For those things which hide within and are extracted are divine. Therefore, divine things are retained by divine things. The soul and the spirit are extracted from divine things, and are retained by them, penetrating the bodies by their quality. For it is the truth: *Nature delights in nature, nature embraces nature, and nature conquers nature.* <note>original: "Natura naturam oblectat, amplectitur, & vincit." This is one of the most famous axioms in alchemy, attributed to Ostanes or Pseudo-Democritus, expressing the sympathy and transformation of materials.</note> For when that which lies hidden within is well-managed and purged by the divine water, it delights in itself, penetrating, conquering, and remaining within its own bodily nature. Recognizing its own nature, it rejoices in the connected and joined alternation.

But to return to the matter: **The four bodies are tinted, and do not tint.** <note>In alchemy, to "tint" means to change the color and nature of a metal. Here, Flamel notes a stage where the materials receive color but cannot yet pass it on to others.</note> For whitened **magnesia** <term>Magnesia: Here referring to the "white" stage of the primary material, a purified mineral substance ready for transformation.</term> does not allow the bodies to be broken, nor the "shadow of **Venus**" <note>The "shadow of Venus" refers to the green or impure copper-like dross that can spoil the purity of the silver or gold stages.</note> to be brought in. But see what the Philosopher <note>likely referring to Hermes Trismegistus or Geber</note> says: that four bodies are tinted and do not tint; nor does he say as before, "and when they are tinted, they tint." Understand the substance of the solid bodies in this way: He says, "Whitened magnesia," etc. What is this magnesia if not the whole composite? How then is it whitened? When it is rightly governed <note>original: "gnbernatur", a typo for "gubernatur"</note> and roasted with the divine thing, having been purged, it brings back the hidden things of all solid bodies from the deep, so that the nature of the bodies may be tinted through them. ### ANNOTATIONS

Irrigated by the **divine water**, it does not allow the bodies to be broken, but rather it takes away their shadow <note>In alchemy, "shadow" refers to the dark, impure, or opaque qualities of a base metal that prevent it from shining like gold.</note> by means of the foam of silver <note>original: "argenti spuma," often referring to litharge or lead oxide.</note> mixed with **magnesia**, and it whitens the body and other things of that same kind. Behold the beauties of **magnesia** and marvel at how it brings forth a multitude through a single thing: some parts indeed from unity, some from duality, some from the ternary, and even from more, it extends until its whole is preserved. Some parts indeed come from the monad <note>The "monad" refers to a single, indivisible unit or the primary substance.</note>, namely the **fiery medicine**; some from duality, as with the body of magnesia itself; some from the ternary, namely the purging with **divine water**; and if it intends anything further, it refers the roasting <note>original: "affationem," a term for the alchemical process of heating or digestion.</note> through others, until the whole sought-after work is kept wiped clean, and is perfected and completed in an absolute way. 

For the material suited for making gold is **mercury** itself. **Mercury** from cinnabar (he says) is sometimes from **Venus** <note>Venus is the alchemical name for copper.</note>, but sometimes indeed from B. <note>The letter "B" likely refers to a specific substance like Borax or a shorthand for a secondary vessel or stage in the process.</note> **Mercury** from cinnabar is **magnesia**; **mercury** from B. is the **divine water**, which is extracted from a constricted instrument <note>A reference to a distillation apparatus or a retort with a narrow neck.</note>, and these are the things which change the matter. 

**Venus**, like a human, has a soul and a body. It is necessary, therefore, to strip the bodies of their matter, so that while the tinting spirit remains, it may be suited for the perfection of whatever is sought. What is **Venus**? That which is the material body in man. The soul indeed is the subtle part within it, which comes forth into the light through the regimen <note>The "regimen" is the controlled sequence of heating and chemical stages in the alchemical process.</note>: that is, the spirit having the power of tinting <note>To "tint" is to project the transformative color and quality of the Elixir onto a base metal.</note>. But the body is heavy and material, and its own earthy part which possesses the shadow, which must be stripped away by the **fiery medicine**. To strip away is to humble, to corrupt, to dissolve, and to mortify <note>To "mortify" means to kill the outward form of a metal so its inner spirit can be released.</note>. Afterward, let nothing be lacking, and nothing remain, except the extraction of the cloud <note>Vapor or steam.</note> and the water.

...the true figure of its use, he called it the **water of vitriol** <note>original: "aquam calcanthi." Vitriol, specifically blue or green sulfates, was a primary material in alchemy used to derive strong mineral acids.</note>: for in that place is the generation of copper: and **vitriol** generates gold. The **flower of copper** <note>original: "flos æris," often referring to verdigris or the refined oxide that forms on the surface of copper.</note> (says the Philosopher) is that very thing which consumes and perfects the matter: it is also the masculine spirit. It is explained thus: Cast forth from the substance drawn from the Most High, so that the divine virtue, having been effected and converted into spirit, may perform the divine work. That which is divine must be sought, so that the matter may be stripped away and become soul alone; then the spirit, seeking the heights, may take on color, and so that it may be more combative and tenacious when facing the fire, like the flat body of spirits <note>original: "demonum," here used in the Neo-Platonic sense of ethereal or intermediary beings rather than evil entities.</note> and penetrating things: for the root of the solid is the plane.

Therefore, once converted into spirit and carried upward like fire (by reason of doctrine), it is in a way assimilated to fire, and becomes more penetrating and tenacious: which principles are indeed found in the nature of the world. For just as the **four elements** are transmuted into one another and become what they were not—since their substance is not contrary to substance—fire becomes earth, and earth becomes water, and water becomes air, and again water becomes earth; and from the earth all the way to the higher regions, all things, changed and dissolved within themselves, perform their work, as it is found in the writings of **Hermes** <note>referring to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary founder of alchemy and author of the Emerald Tablet.</note>: For the qualities are contrary to one another, but the substances are not. Thus also when the composite becomes earth, it becomes water, and from water air, and from air fire. Just as, therefore, from the heavens—which rest upon the earth like a vaulted chamber—all these things arise from exhalations, so also from the **pot** <note>original: "olla," the base vessel used for heating.</note> as if from the earth, and the **alembic** <note>original: "alembico," the lid or "still-head" used in distillation.</note> placed above it as if from the heavens, violent mutations occur. And just as the putrefactions of the earth, exhaling, are transmuted, so also the **flower of copper** is simultaneously transmuted as it exhales. You shall also consider this same thing in the human head, and in the **cucurbits** <note>original: "cucurbitulæ," gourd-shaped glass vessels used in distillation; the text here draws an analogy between the human skull and the alchemical vessel.</note>,

### ANNOTATIONS.

...of the cucurbit <note>A gourd-shaped vessel used in distillation.</note>, or placed upon the body like a helmet <note>The "helmet" or *galea* refers to the lid of a distilling apparatus, which catches the rising vapors.</note>. As it was not said in vain, "therefore grind it": so now it is not rashly said, "they are cooked gradually, and they are raised up among themselves." For by a light **boiling** <term>elixation|a gentle cooking or boiling process used to extract medicinal or spiritual essences</term>, the tinting **spirit** that lies hidden is drawn out: that which is carried upward is the spirit free of the body, and the **soul** of the whole compound, that is, of the body. 

Just as the soul reveals itself to bodies, **fermenting** <note>original: "fermentans." In alchemy, fermentation is the stage where a substance transforms the whole mass into its own nature, much like leaven in bread.</note> all things, having within itself three active substances—motion, sense, and ignition—so also this **fiery spirit**, having been made a soul free of the body, when it has penetrated the body of the thing that tints everything, if it is established within it like a plane in a solid, through the injection of its medicine into silver thinned by fire. This joyful fiery spirit (he says) leaps in, which also lies hidden in bodies, and seeking the heights, it also retains the body itself as its own soul. 

Furthermore, as for what concerns the different cause of the extracted water, let this not frighten you: for it is the flower that is extracted, not just once, but twice three times through the "sea-seekers" <note>original: "maripetas." Likely a technical term for specific vessels or specialized workers in the alchemical process.</note>. After it has been shown according to its properties, the flower is the undiluted **water of sulfur**, and it has the use of **fixation** <term>fixation|the process of making a volatile substance stable and resistant to fire</term>. 

For the Philosopher said, "even a little gum will tint every body." **Mary** <note>Referring to Mary the Prophetess, a legendary foundational figure in alchemy credited with inventing the "bain-marie" or water bath.</note> says: "The restraint of all things that flee from the fire is the whitest **alabaster** of all, the **brain-stone** <note>original: "lapis encephalus." A symbolic stone associated with the 'head' or the most refined part of the matter.</note>, hidden in the mind and having a kind of heat: taking this, grind it, and mix it with vinegar, and throwing it into a small vessel, finally hide it under dung <note>Dung was commonly used in alchemical laboratories as a source of steady, gentle heat for long-term digestion.</note>: for this is what is sought—namely, that the undiluted **water of sulfur** and the **rust** <note>original: "rubiginem." Often referring to the red tincture or the final state of the matter.</note> are one and the same; and here truly turns the hinge of the entire art." Next, in these things, it is considered in a single composition...

# NICOLAI FLAMELLI

...lest all things become both that which tints and that which is tinted; that which flees and that which follows; that which is familiar and that which is contrary; male and female; groom and bride; the active and the passive. For there is but **one serpent** <note>The serpent, or Ouroboros, is a central alchemical symbol representing the unity of matter and the cyclic nature of the work.</note>, which possesses two compositions and a certain rust <note>original: "rubiginem." This refers to the "redness" or oxidation/corrosion of the metal which, in alchemy, often signals a stage of transformation.</note>. One is the **Whole** <note>original: "Vnum est totum," reflecting the Greek "Hen to Pan," a foundational Hermetic maxim meaning "The All is One."</note>, on account of which all things exist; and if the Whole did not contain the Whole, nothing would be the Whole, as the great **Chimes** <note>likely referring to Zosimos of Panopolis, a 3rd-century alchemist often cited in Latin texts as "Chimes" or "Chemes."</note> says. 

For making things **fixed** <term>fixation|the process of making a volatile substance stable and resistant to fire</term> is the property not only of sulfur, but also of those things that have been whitened, softened, and thinned out. For when substances are acted upon by **white sulfur** in the stages of perfection and roasting <note>original: "assatione," a slow heating process.</note>, then they themselves are turned into **incombustible sulfur**; they remain doing what they have undergone, through a process of giving and receiving. Then both substances, having been made into sulfur, render the materials "fixed"—that is, they ensure they cannot be broken apart from one another. 

Next, he asks: why do you seek out so many different species of things, when **one nature** overcomes all? If you weigh this nature carefully from the beginning to the end, you will see the mystery made manifest: that is, the **cymbalia** <note>original: "cymbalia." In this context, it may refer to the "resonance" or the harmonious ringing-out of the completed work, or a specific type of alchemical vessel.</note>. O natures, creators of the celestial natures! These, once dissolved, perform all the operations. 

What then does it mean "to dissolve"? Come here to the liquids and learn: Take the **saffron of Cilicia** <note>Crocus of Cilicia; alchemists often used botanical names as "Decknamen" (cover-names) for chemical minerals.</note>, joined together with the flower of the **safflower** <note>original: "cinici," likely a corruption of "cnici" (Cnicus or Carthamus tinctorius).</note> and the juice of the vine, and in a single sequence of liquids you will find the dissolution. Come here again to the **white** [work]: and likewise within it is the dissolution, as it teaches you itself, saying: "Leave it below, and it shall be done." 

He spoke truly: some have called these "bodies," others "sulfur," and they were not deceived. And because of this, error has spread throughout the world on account of the multitude of names. Those things that are said concerning the four and five elements are to be referred to those same names. They have placed **Saturn** <note>Alchemical name for Lead.</note> [here], and opposite to it, lead in the high... <meta>tifsimo (altissimo)</meta>

It explains the role of sulfur in stabilizing substances and warns that the confusing variety of names used by alchemists all refer to the same underlying process.</summary>

### ANNOTATIONS

...at the highest peak, and after Jupiter himself, come Mars, the Sun, Venus, Mercury, and in the lowest place, the Moon. Though distinct from one another, they are joined to a single orb <note>original: "vni tamen orbi coniunguntur." This reflects the geocentric model where planets occupy concentric spheres unified by the "One Spirit."</note>. One spirit serves them and governs these same things; they are loved because of this one spirit, they look upon one another with mutual eyes, they move together, they dissolve one another, and they walk within their own dwellings, just as the most high Creator himself established. Similarly, these things are found on earth; they are in the air, in the water, and in the fire. They possess total peace among themselves, and a single craftsman <note>original: "opifex," referring to the Demiurge or God as the Great Architect.</note> serves and rules them. All things are joined under a single yoke and drink from a single potion: for they are nourished from the earth, and each of them performs its own duty, remains in its own seat, and obeys the will of the Creator. Each of them is hidden within the earth and in its own glory.

And you, O friends, when you wish to approach this **most beautiful art** <note>The "Art" is a standard term for Alchemy.</note>, observe the nature of herbs and from where they come: some from the mountains, others from the valleys, and others from the fields. Consider the air that serves them; consider the **divine water** <term>divine water|Often 'theia hydor' in Greek, meaning both 'divine water' and 'sulfur water,' a key agent in transmutation</term> that bedews them, bringing the mist and governing all other things. When these are mixed together, they are one, and no one can separate them once they have united into a single nature. 

"O **Cleopatra**," said **Ostanes** <note>Ostanes was a legendary Persian magus, and Cleopatra the Alchemist was a 3rd-century author; they appear here as archetypal teachers in a fictionalized dialogue.</note>, "the whole secret lies hidden within you. Explain clearly to us about the elements: how the higher descends to the lower, how the middle is near to the highest and the outermost, and how the parts do not clash even when they precede and are unified. What is the medium between them that makes them one? And how do the **blessed waters** <note>original: "aquæ benedictæ." In alchemy, this refers to the mercurial solvent or 'spirit' that purifies the 'dead' base metals.</note> descend to see the dead lying there, bound and placed..."

<summary>This page describes the harmony of the macrocosm and microcosm, urging students to study nature's unity before concluding with a dialogue where Ostanes asks Cleopatra to reveal the secrets of elemental transformation.</summary>

# OF NICHOLAS FLAMEL

...risen in darkness, and in gloom within the underworld <note>original: "inferno." In alchemy, the "underworld" or "hell" usually refers to the bottom of the flask or the state of "blackness" (nigredo) where matter is broken down.</note>, and how the **medicine of life** enters and awakens them, so that they might wake as if from sleep. It shows how the new waters enter—those which are generated in the beginning of the bed <note>original: "lecti." This likely refers to the "bed" of the chemical matter or the base of the furnace.</note> and within the bed itself—and how it carries the clouds themselves, and how a watery cloud rises from the sea. 

The *Philosophers*, contemplating those things which were shown, rejoice. But *Cleopatra* says to them: "The waters entering the bodies awaken the spirits shut within them, which were weakened, as if from sleep. Again," she says, "they have endured hardships, and again they shall be shut up in the underworld; but little by little they are born, and they rise, and they put on diverse and glorious colors just like the flowers of spring, which rejoice and exult because of the beauty with which they are clothed. To you," I say, "O wise ones, when you dig up herbs, elements, and stones from their places, they seem beautiful enough, and yet not truly beautiful: because **fire** tests all things. 

But when they put on glory through fire and the **green color** <note>original: "colorem viridem." The "Green Lion" or the appearance of green often symbolizes the first sign of life or "vegetative soul" returning to the dead matter.</note>, then there are greater spectacles; there is hidden glory and beauty sought with care, and the earth changed into **divinity** <term>divinity|The Latin "divinitas" here implies a state of perfection or incorruptibility, such as the Philosopher's Stone.</term>: because fire nourishes it. Just as an infant nourished in the womb grows little by little, and when the appointed month arrives, is not prevented from coming forth into the light, even so is this remarkable thing. 

Storms and waves strike it in the underworld and in the tomb in which they lie; but when the tomb is opened, it rises from the underworld like an infant from the womb." The *Philosophers* contemplate this beauty, just as a devoted mother gazes at the infant she has birthed. Alchemical arsenic was often not the modern element but a volatile, "masculine" principle or a specific mineral used to whiten metals.</note>, and whiten it divinely. Behold, brothers, the admirable secret, utterly unknown: behold, the truth has been revealed to you. # NICOLAI FLAMELLI

[...our] <note>The text begins mid-word with "stra," likely "nostra" (our). "Our Ethiopia" is a common alchemical metaphor for the "black stage" of the work, where the matter is purified.</note> Ethiopia, from which are taken the herbs, stones, and divine bodies that God and heaven have made: for in each one of them the Creator has sown a specific power. One He made green, another not; one dry, one moist, one joining, another separating; one retaining, and another receding, and in their mutual meeting they hold one another fast. One [nature] leads back into the body within another, in another it adorns, and they become a single nature which embraces and retains all other natures. And that one thing overcomes every nature of fire and earth, and alters all their power: behold the end. 

When it is perfected, it becomes a **lethal medicine** <note>original: "medicina lethalis." While "lethal" usually means deadly, in alchemy this refers to a "penetrating" or "all-consuming" tincture that "kills" the base properties of a metal to allow for its transformation into gold.</note> flowing through the body: for in a certain way it enters by its own color and insinuates itself into bodies. For through the process of **putrefaction** <note>The stage where the substance "dies" and rots, turning black, before being reborn.</note> and heat, it becomes a flowing medicine that enters every body without hindrance or harm. Here, indeed, the art of philosophy has received its final touch.

Therefore, there are many things that make gold, but that which is truly made is one. First: there is a certain shore-sand, which because of its golden color is called **chrysites** <note>original: "chrysites." A term often used for litharge (lead monoxide) or other golden-colored minerals.</note>; some call it golden sand. Let it be ground in a mortar, thickened with salt, and heated by fire for a day and a night. Once ground again, let it be washed with salt water, and the powder be soaked with vinegar drop by drop so that it combines; let it be dried and soaked again, and do this a fourth time. Melting both silver and lead together, cast in the powder; let them be fused together and mixed. Afterwards, removing it and cooling it for several hours, you will see the whole mass is hard: then, cleaning away the sand, you will find gold.

# ANNOTATIONS.

[...Sul]phur <note>The text begins mid-word, likely "Sulphur vivum" or native sulfur, a key reagent in alchemy representing the active, masculine principle.</note> vive, and grinding cinnabar <note>A bright red mineral, mercury(II) sulfide, often associated with the color of the "Philosopher's Stone."</note> together with it, and making a sticky mixture, cast it into a pure glass vessel with a narrow mouth. Once sealed with clay, let it be roasted by fire for a day. Afterward, removing the clay, you will find a dry material with a thickness similar to pitch. Grinding this again, store it in an earthen vessel; placing the whole thing in the fire and removing the lid, you will find it has turned yellow, etc.

And if indeed you take white magnesia <note>In alchemy, "magnesia" was not just magnesium oxide but a broad term for various transformative minerals or the "crude matter" of the stone.</note> and an equal weight of best-prepared sand. Afterward, grinding both together, if you cook them in radish oil, you will have that which is considered gold in the furnaces. But if it is not shining in color, let the things mentioned above be anointed with salt and ground with **mysy** <term>Mysy: An ancient term for a metallic vitriol or sulfate ore, believed to have coloring properties.</term> and **saffron of iron** <note>original: "ferri croco." A reddish-brown iron oxide, often called rust, used as a pigment or alchemical reagent.</note>. By these things communicating their powers, you will make gold more brilliant than the golden sands of the **Pactolus** <note>A river in ancient Lydia (modern Turkey) famous for the gold dust found in its waters, said to be the source of King Midas's wealth.</note>.

If indeed you have gold and wish to double its weight without losing any quality, weighing this gold, weigh out twice as much of the drugs: **mysy** and filings of beryl, so that they are four times the amount of the gold composed of both. Mixing or tempering these, attach them to the gold; and when you have cast them into a **crucible** <note>A ceramic or metal container used for melting substances at very high temperatures.</note> and made it red-hot, draw it out, and you will become twice as rich as you were.

But our purpose was not to deal with the brightening or increasing of gold, but with its creation. This, therefore, I shall provide, although my discourse has wandered somewhat to look at other arts in passing.

Therefore, cinnabar and the golden "flower of copper" <note>likely verdigris or brass filings</note>, like certain natural forms cast into **lunar matter** <note>A reference to silver, which is associated with the Moon in alchemical symbolism.</note>, create a golden body. If, therefore, melting silver, you temper it by mixing in the medicines, the **Sun** <note>Gold</note> will become yours from the **Moon** <note>Silver</note>; and if you cut into such a thing, you will find the color, and for every use of gold, the silver turned into gold. And the Moon indeed makes gold, while the Sun [makes] lu[na]... <note>The sentence ends mid-word at the bottom of the page.</note>

# NICOLAS FLAMEL

For it does not produce [the light]; for it alone receives its light from that source: and it serves not only the moon <note>In alchemy, the Moon represents silver.</note>, but also the great light <note>The Sun, representing gold.</note>. Therefore, drawing out the body of copper <note>original: "Æris."</note> as much as is proper, make it like a certain long tongue. Afterward, placing it over the coals, urge it into the furnace <note>original: "vulcanum," referring to Vulcan, the god of fire.</note> itself, irradiating it now with rock salt, now with Attic ochre <note>A high-quality yellow earth pigment from the region of Athens.</note> continually, or alternately adorning the shoulder and breast of the **Paphian** <note>original: "Paphiæ," an epithet for Venus, the goddess traditionally associated with the metal copper.</note>; she will clearly become more beautiful, and shedding her grayish-blue color, she will appear entirely golden. Truly, since we mentioned the **regimen** <note>The "regimen" refers to the precise control of the heat and duration of the fire in the alchemical process.</note> above, we shall interpret what that name intends. And because we require a tincture of <term>chrysitis</term> <note>A gold-colored litharge or mineral used for coloring.</note> for the works, it is first necessary to have the medicine, so that we may use it at the proper time.

<term>Chrysocolla</term> <note>Literally "gold-glue"; historically used for malachite or borax used in soldering gold.</note> is a flower of the earth, born in *Macedonia*; cleansing this with fresh water, and afterward drying it in the rays of the sun, dissolve and mix it together with <term>flower of Scythian copper</term> and <term>chrysoletus</term> <note>Likely "chrysolite" or a similar yellow-tinted gemstone/mineral.</note>. You have at once both the meaning of the <term>regimen</term> and that which produces the tincture. Truly, you may make gold in this way: when you have melted lead with fire, you shall sprinkle <term>native sulfur</term> <note>original: "sulphur viuum," or "living sulfur," naturally occurring sulfur that has not been processed by heat.</note> upon it, and let it be burned by the fire until the vapor has vanished. Afterward, taking an equal weight of <term>split alum</term> <note>original: "aluminis scissilis," a crystalline form of alum that peels in layers.</note> and <term>cinnabar</term> <note>Mercury(II) sulfide, the primary ore of mercury, valued for its deep red color.</note>, and mixing them in <term>oxymel</term> <note>A medicinal mixture of vinegar and honey.</note>, sprinkle it upon the melted lead, and likewise the <term>native sulfur</term> (as mentioned above) so that it may then become solid, and also receive the color through all its pores; because of all these things, gold may be produced.

->### ANNOTATIONS.<-

...of truth <note>original: "ritæ," likely the conclusion of "veritæ" or "integritæ" from the preceding page.</note> we shall briefly reveal wisdom; shall we leave nothing within the **inner sanctuaries**? <note>original: "adytis," referring to the most private or sacred part of a temple.</note> But neither those who initiate others into the sacred rites say this, nor does mystical divination. The appointed times for the sacred rites have stirred the leader, and again he has rested from his ecstatic frenzy <note>original: "debacchatione," a reference to the wild, inspired state of a follower of Bacchus.</note>, and being initiated into the mysteries, he has hastened back composed. But do not be vexed if I, another leader of the sacred rites, sit before you, who are the first and leader of the mysteries. For more excellent things have been entrusted to you, and you bring down God from heaven, and you elevate the soul and the mind, which is currently bound in matter, and you apply it to that which is exempt from matter. I, however, lie upon the ground and am a contemplator of nature, for I have not yet returned to the contemplation of invisible things, because a sharper light has not yet been granted to me. These are the words of **Psellus** <note>Michael Psellus (c. 1017–1078), a Byzantine monk, philosopher, and historian known for his writings on alchemy and Neoplatonism.</note> to **Xiphilinus the Patriarch** <note>Johannes Xiphilinus, Patriarch of Constantinople and a contemporary of Psellus.</note>, and further. You know then what we shall do, he says? To me indeed the inner sanctuaries of the earth are entrusted; you, however, have attained those heavenly things. Let us therefore share with each other the things we have: you to me the remarkable spectacles of the divine, and I to you the natural effects of the world. But you see what I have done for you: for when I opened the springs of gold, I neither shook **Mount Athos** nor moved **Mount Pangaeum** <note>Mountains in Greece and Macedonia famous in antiquity for their gold and silver mines.</note>, nor did I open certain subterranean veins, but by rubbing stones together and mixing certain herbs, I have frankly and usefully crafted for you gold of great price. Devise something of this kind for me also—not stirring the air, nor lifting me on high, but in some way show me the **super-celestial good** here on earth: for it is nowhere, yet everywhere, and in every part of the universe. The discovery remains hidden from many; that which is within us, we wanderers seek in the heavens. But teach me how it is near, and how it is far...

<meta>This page continues the address from Michael Psellus to the Patriarch Xiphilinus, concluding the philosophical appeal before transitioning into a summary of alchemical allegories attributed to Democritus. # THE WORKS OF NICOLAS FLAMEL

...is further away, not distant by location, but approaching closer in its disposition, that which is [otherwise] further off. Tell me, what is the reason for the descent of the intelligence? And then, having descended so far, how does it ascend again? In short, lead me by the hand to God. For if it be by a path narrow enough, I shall be content; but if it be by a path capable of holding us both, I shall be even more so. If you lead me to be initiated into these sacred rites, I promise you that I will overlook no remaining work of science or nature, and no kind of study, whether of ancient wisdom or of the secret arts; but to you, if it pleases you, I will explain those things that are beneath the earth. 

But indeed, if I bestow this service upon you most zealously, and you on your part do not show me the knowledge, you exchange bronze things for golden ones to my disadvantage <note>original: "ærea pro aureis," a classical reference to a lopsided trade, often citing Glaucus and Diomedes in the Iliad.</note>; nor yet on that account will I be angry, nor will I brand you with the shameful mark of an ungrateful mind. Instead, I shall simply know that the Attic <note>Attic refers to Athens, implying refined, clear, or intellectual light.</note> solar light is kindled more purely in glass. Do you wish me then to say what I say for all? Love more fervently, and farewell.

# ANNOTATIONS

...by ascending, and by gazing upon the great celestial ornament. When you have contemplated the adornment of these things, lifting your mind above all things, and considering the great and sufficiently shining glory of the angels and such great joy, do not turn back thereafter to the material notion of this humble and lowly science of making gold <note>original: "auri conficiendi"; this refers to the alchemical art of transmutation, which the author views as inferior to spiritual enlightenment.</note> which has now been laid bare. Instead, lift yourself entirely toward higher things, and raising the wings of the mind to the splendor of the supernal substances, gazing with intent and internal eyes upon the innumerable and lofty beauties of these things, and upon another light which surpasses all light. 

Admiring this, and setting aside all works in the world, and contemplating in your mind an elected death before its time <note>A "philosophical death," referring to the ascetic practice of dying to worldly desires while still alive to achieve spiritual union.</note>, and mortifying your body with you: furthermore, indeed, denying your own soul, glorifying and praising the King and Lord of glory of all things with clear hymns: struck indeed with trembling and fear, contemplate in your mind the omnipotent power, and His goodness which surpasses all goodness, and how all things were brought forth through His unspeakable wisdom. 

The tongue is numbed, and the mind also cannot bear to either fully investigate or recount what great works <note>original: "magnalia"</note> of Almighty God are truly laid up for the contemplating mind. Considering all these things within ourselves, let us also strive so that with everyone—as brothers through faith and delight—we may be united and made like, as much as possible, to the Word of God who loved us. And worshipping God Himself our Lord, let us love Him with our whole soul, our whole mind, and our whole heart: and having admired such a great treasure, let us glorify Him, so that at last, having been deemed worthy of the companionship of God, through...

<meta>This page concludes the spiritual exhortation from the previous page and begins a practical alchemical recipe attributed to the Byzantine scholar Michael Psellus, as found in the works of Nicholas Flamel. It transitions from divine contemplation to the physical "work" of the laboratory.</meta>

# THE WORKS OF NICHOLAS FLAMEL

...that we may be filled with grace itself, and with the light of His wisdom, which illustrates all nature and illuminates every mind. To Him be praise forever.

These things Xiphilinus the Patriarch wrote to Michael Psellus, who in return replied: Take one part of chrysolite <note>original: "chrysolithus"; here identified by the commentator as arsenic.</note>, and three parts of the magnesia of cinnabar: grind them until they are joined together and the substances are mixed, and no more "living sulfur" <note>Sulfur in its active, combustible, and "raw" state.</note> appears: for it becomes very black, like writing ink. After three days, casting it into a bath, add an equal portion of the liquid that is used for washing, and wash away all the tincture, catching it, and so forth.

Chrysolite is arsenic, as you know, or the Aethesian stone. Magnesia of cinnabar is chalcopyrite <note>A copper-based mineral.</note>, and it is also called lead. The liquid for the bath is the "rooted vinegar" <note>original: "acetum radicatum"; a potent, distilled vinegar used as a powerful solvent in alchemy.</note> of our radish root with its own salt, drawn from it by <term>cohobation</term> <note>The process of repeatedly distilling a liquid by pouring it back over the solid remains (the "dregs") to concentrate its strength.</note> into a dissolving solution. Wash it, he says, until its redness <note>original: "rubigo"; literally "rust," referring to the reddish impurities or the surface oxidation of the metal.</note> departs, and thus nothing of the copper remains in the lead. This is the "great solution" by evacuation, the rusting, the blackening, the washing, and so forth. Proceed as you know how. 

Taking the mentioned "evacuation," leave it to settle in vessels, and pressing out the liquid, dry it. That which settles at the bottom, you will find to be like writing ink; grind this until it becomes perfectly lemon-colored, or white first: in this manner, let it be ground while drying over a continuous fire, until it whitens and becomes brilliantly white. Take care, therefore, that you do not grow weary during the time of whitening <note>original: "dealbatio"; the alchemical stage of Albedo, where the "black" matter is purified and turned white.</note>: for negligence often happens because that beauty is not yet visible: since through this whitening, the copper becomes "without shadow," casting off all its earthly excess and coarseness. If

<meta>The previous page concluded with a description of the "great solution" and the washing of the material. This page continues the practical instructions, moving from the "blackening" and "washing" phases to the "whitening" (dealbatio) and the final "tingeing" or transformation into gold or silver.</meta>

->### ANNOTATIONS.<-

Therefore the copper <note>original: "æs"; in alchemy, this can refer to copper or a base alloy.</note> is whitened, it becomes spiritual, and finally nothing is left, nothing is missing, except for the rising of the mist and the water. Zosimus <note>Zosimus of Panopolis, a prominent Greco-Egyptian alchemist from the 3rd century AD.</note> says: I know of one order, having two works: first, that it should flow through the resin (understand this as through its purgatory); later, that the moisture of Saturn <note>The alchemical symbol and name for Lead.</note> may be dried out by rising into a light powder. Therefore, place an equal part of the light stone with the purified substance, and grind it by washing and emptying it with vinegar extracted through an <term>alembic</term> <note>A distillation apparatus consisting of two vessels connected by a tube.</note>, until it whitens, until it becomes bright. Observe, therefore, the time of whitening, as mentioned above, etc.

Thus, sweetening and drying the medicine <note>The "medicine" refers to the elixir or the transformative substance being prepared.</note>, set it aside, and balance the medicine: three parts of <term>Calcanthus</term> <note>Vitriol; specifically copper or iron sulfate.</note>, one part of magnesia, and another part of the medicine; grind them together, moistening them in the sun with white vinegar for six days, and afterwards, having dried it, place it in cow dung <note>A common alchemical method for providing a gentle, steady, and long-lasting heat (incubation).</note>, and allow them to be burnt for two or three days. Upon taking it from the fire, you will find gold tinged like blood: this is the cinnabar <note>original: "cinabaris"; here referring to the "Cinnabar of the Philosophers," a high-stage red powder, rather than common ore.</note> of the Philosophers, and the bright lemon-yellow copper. 

"Why do you care," says Senior <note>Senior Zadith, the Latinized name of the 10th-century Arabic alchemist Ibn Umayl.</note> "for a forest of many things? Since there is one natural matter, and one nature, which overcomes all." Then, therefore, dissolved and fermented by the divine water <note>A symbolic term for a potent solvent or "mercurial" water.</note>, like the leaven of bread, and thereafter even with chrysolite <note>Defined on the previous page as arsenic or the Aethesian stone.</note> ground in equal parts and dissolved in water, according to its nature through fluxion, it tinges every body. 

Zosimus says: The natural Sun <note>The alchemical name for Gold.</note>, having been made spiritual through chrysolite, tinges according to nature. Also, if we dissolve silver by water, and make it spiritual through chrysolite, it tinges copper white. For he also said this in other words. For the two tinctures <note>The red tincture (for gold) and the white tincture (for silver).</note> differ from each other in nothing else, except only in color: that is, one and the same prepara-

<summary>This page details the chemical processes of whitening and tingeing metals using vinegar, vitriol, and "divine water." It cites authorities like Zosimus and Senior Zadith to explain how base copper is transformed into "spiritual" gold or silver.</summary>

--- Page 218 ---
<meta>This page continues the specific instructions for the final transformation of the alchemical medicine. The previous page ended mid-sentence with "you will find gold tinge," which this page completes by explaining the underlying logic and final results of the process.</meta>

# ANNOTATIONS OF NICOLAS FLAMEL

they have their purpose: because they are first ground down by the divine water, and afterwards truly become a spiritual medicine through chrysolite <note>original: "chrysolitum"; a yellowish-green gemstone often associated with the sun and spiritual clarity in alchemical texts.</note>. Each one tinges according to its own nature: the sun <note>Sol, the alchemical symbol for Gold.</note> tinges gold, and the moon <note>Luna, the alchemical symbol for Silver.</note> tinges silver. By "divine water," understand the visitation of hidden things. Therefore, the Etesian stone <note>original: "Ætesius lapis"; likely referring to the Aetite or "Eagle-stone," a stone believed to have been found in eagles' nests and possessed of magical and medicinal properties.</note> bound with copper and reduced with oil of radish—this made the scholars of Egypt blessed. Therefore, raising the composition by art and calling forth the latent nature, you will finally, through that nature, create the formations of bodies; and thus, bodies are rendered incorporeal, and the incorporeal become corporeal. If all things are thinned by fire and the soot is carried away, they will be diverse and unbreakable bodies.

->Praised be the<-
->Most High.<-

->T H E  E N D.<-
